## 11.16c vNext predictive relationship graph, July 23, 2026

The affected assets are A02 private memory, A03 durable agent state, and A07 provenance evidence across F03/F06/F12 and B06/B09/B16. Typed relationship assertion and transition derivation create a poisoning target: a caller could attach another user's evidence, relabel similarity or sequence as causality, replay an edge into greater authority, fan out an unbounded graph, or leak private claim content through diagnostics and logs.

**Closed in source.** `vnext-transition-graph.ts` is the canonical P2 mutation and derivation boundary. Canonical edge writes validate both endpoint claims and every cited source ref under the same writable User principal, require bounded certainty and non-empty bounded provenance, use a stable replay key, and encode semantic, evidence, temporal, causal, and consolidation classes independently. Derived edges require method/version metadata. Database constraints require causal edges to remain `causal_hypothesis`; model-proposed `sourceClaimIndex` produces only an evidence-backed semantic `qualifies` hypothesis. Transition recompute is transaction-locked, bounded, stable-keyed, and deletes only stale rows from its exact method/version. Its observed paths derive only from explicit temporal edges; an optional mechanism is included only from an evidence-traceable causal hypothesis. Every table is owner/account scoped, every read uses visible scope, every write/delete uses writable scope, diagnostics are bounded, and structured logs contain IDs and discriminants rather than claim or source content.

Controls: DATA-01, DATA-04, AGENT-02, AGENT-05, OBS-01, REC-02. Rollback is the merged PR revert; additive columns/tables can remain inert. Residual risk: source-backed causal hypotheses can still be wrong. P3/P4 must keep prediction output shadow-only, measure calibration and reviewed precision, and abstain before any user-facing activation.

## 11.16a vNext memory signal integrity repair, July 23, 2026

The affected assets are A02 personal memory, A03 durable agent state, A07 audit evidence, and A08 resource integrity across F03/F06/F12 and B06/B09/B16. Rollback is the merged PR revert; the additive event tables may remain inert. Residual risk: P0 records typed strength events but does not yet activate a strength projection in retrieval. That controlled activation belongs to the next orthogonal-dimensions phase.

## 11.19 Speech-recognition environment binding and attempt identity, July 29, 2026

**Status:** Foundation and unified Scribe/Deepgram meeting transport controls complete; Speechmatics activation remains intentionally gated.  
**Severity:** High before controls; Low residual while source policy continues constraining Recall/native routing to Scribe/Deepgram.  
**Owner:** Speech Recognition / Platform Environment.  
**SLA:** Before any Speechmatics activation or database binding is allowed to route meeting audio.

**Assets/data:** A01 identity, A02/S1 raw meeting audio and S2 transcript, A03 meeting state, A05 provider credentials, A07 operational evidence, A08 realtime availability. **Flows:** F02, F03, F06, F12. **Boundaries:** B03, B04, B05, B06, B08, B14, B16.

**Threat:** An arbitrary or cross-environment capability binding could select the wrong provider account, alter raw-audio egress, expose a credential through client/session/log state, merge acoustic speakers across reconnects, or turn shared-microphone chronology into Person identity.

**Deterministic controls:** `server/speech-recognition/` owns a strict versioned, discriminated allowlist for Scribe, Deepgram, and Speechmatics configs. The typed system-permission-gated Platform Environment route is the only speech mutation boundary; the generic capability API excludes speech writes and reads. A binding-aware resolver joins the exact environment, binding, and active matching Provider Connection before decryption, rejects binding-owned secrets, and accepts only global/system platform-managed credentials. Runtime adapters receive credentials explicitly, current messages retain secret-free immutable attempt/binding/model/config provenance, and machine speaker keys are scoped to random provider-session attempt IDs. Native shared microphones remain audio sources only: creators stay roster participants but are not bound to the microphone or first cluster. One `SpeechRecognitionStreamCoordinator` now owns Recall and native candidate startup, protocol readiness, a 512 KiB/5-second audio budget, visible overflow failure, serialized final delivery, same-binding reconnect with a fresh attempt ID, no post-commit provider switching, and bounded provider/consumer drain. Enabled environment bindings supersede the rolling legacy secret for their exact adapter kind; the legacy candidate is projected only when no enabled binding exists, so configuration cannot hide an unreported second credential path. Source policy still selects only Scribe for participant streams and Deepgram for shared rooms during migration. Scribe emits an explicit commit before closing; Deepgram emits `CloseStream` and waits for terminal metadata/server close. Safe logs exclude audio, transcript, credentials, provider error bodies, and close reasons.

**Rollout evidence/prerequisites:** Migration `0106_speech_recognition_foundation.sql` and boot convergence add only `sort_order` plus an index; old meeting documents remain readable because all provenance fields are optional. Stage and Live routing remain unchanged until the coordinator rollout begins reading enabled environment bindings. Before activation, create platform-managed Provider Connections, configure Stage bindings through the typed API, keep Speechmatics disabled, verify protocol/config without content-bearing logs, and preserve Live priority until same-audio quality evidence clears the promotion gate.

**Residual risk:** Speechmatics has config/control-plane representation but no implemented registry adapter yet, so selection fails closed. Source-policy adapter constraints deliberately prevent an enabled Speechmatics binding from changing current Scribe/Deepgram meeting routing in this step; the next implementation step must add that adapter before controlled Stage activation. Production build proves compilation, not real provider timing or tail-drain behavior, so authenticated Stage acceptance remains required. Functional and quality promotion evidence remain pending by design.

## 11.20 Consented retained meeting-audio and Stage replay, July 29, 2026

**Status:** Privacy-safe foundation complete; no sample exists without explicit versioned owner consent, and provider promotion remains evidence-gated.  
**Severity:** Critical before controls; Low residual while retention is native-capture opt-in and replay is Stage/system-permission gated.  
**Owner:** Meetings / Speech Recognition / Object Storage.  
**SLA:** Controls are release-blocking before any raw meeting-audio retention or replay is enabled.

**Assets/data:** A01 identity, A02/S1 raw meeting audio and S2 transcript/evaluation output, A03 meeting and consent state, A05 provider credentials, A07 audit evidence, A08 realtime/storage availability. **Flows:** F02, F03, F06, F12. **Boundaries:** B03, B04, B05, B06, B08, B14, B16.

**Threat:** A caller could retain audio without informed consent, hide recording state after navigation, export or delete another user/Vault's sample, keep raw audio indefinitely, exhaust memory/storage, replay into Live or an arbitrary provider credential, overwrite the canonical transcript with evaluation output, leak plaintext audio/transcripts through object storage or logs, or race deletion against an active evaluation.

**Deterministic controls:** The native creation route defaults retention off and accepts it only with `retentionConsent=true`, consent version 1, and a 1–7 day horizon before the audio WebSocket opens. The consent dialog names raw audio, encryption, evaluation purpose, export/delete, and the seven-day bound; canonical Meeting metadata visibly projects recording/ready/failure state across reconnects. `meeting_audio_samples` and `meeting_audio_evaluations` structurally require user scope, owner, account, Vault, fixed PCM format, bounded size/duration, explicit lifecycle status, and replay-safe uniqueness. `meeting/audio-retention.ts` is the sole mutation boundary: it retains only PCM accepted by live recognition, caps capture at 32 MiB/15 minutes, application-encrypts bytes with AES-256-GCM before the canonical Vault-aware private object upload, verifies object persistence, and records original immutable recognition attempts. Owner export scopes the row before object access, decrypts server-side, verifies byte count, returns private/no-store WAV, and never mints a storage URL. Owner delete and canonical Meeting delete remove evaluation objects, sample objects, ACLs, and then tombstone rows; active evaluation blocks deletion. Hourly expiry discovers 25 metadata rows under the named `meeting-audio-expiry` principal, then restores each exact owner/account/Vault before deletion. Replay requires `system:write`, one explicit visible environment named Stage, one exact enabled binding resolved through the canonical binding-aware credential boundary, fixed shared-room format, bounded pacing/write deadlines and utterance count, and a replay-safe idempotency key. Replay output is a separate application-encrypted artifact carrying original and replay provenance; it never calls `ingestMeetingEvent(...)` or mutates meeting messages. Logs contain only IDs, counts, timings, status/failure discriminants, and safe error types—never audio, transcript text, provider bodies, speaker labels, credentials, object URLs, or raw keys in client state.

**Rollback and residual risk:** Revert the merged PR; additive tables can remain inert and encrypted objects remain owner-deletable/expiry-bound. The retained-sample advisory transaction lock serializes replay claims against deletion, replay idempotency converges through its owner/account uniqueness constraint, and deletion tombstones rows before object I/O so partial storage failure remains retryable without making bytes eligible for a new replay. Canonical object-upload logs retain only byte count, category, and Vault-scope state rather than private raw object keys. Application encryption currently buffers a bounded sample in memory for finalization/export/replay, so the 32 MiB ceiling is a hard operational control rather than streaming encryption. The first release retains only explicitly started native shared-room capture; Recall retention requires a separate consent UX and transport review. A completed evaluation proves functionality, not comparative quality. Speechmatics/other provider promotion still requires manually labeled same-audio evidence for speaker confusion, switch errors, cluster count, transcript completeness, latency, and unknown-speaker rate.

## 11.18 Plan and Workflow child retry fencing, July 25, 2026

A03 durable orchestration state, A04 autonomous mutation authority, and A08 availability/cost cross F05 and B11 when a parent monitor times out, loses, or observes a prematurely failed child session. The confirmed defect allowed the parent to request abort, project the child as failed, and start a successor while `AgentExecutor` could still own the prior mutating run. This enabled duplicate People/profile mutations, contradictory audit entries, replay amplification, and false terminal state.

**Closed in source.** `child-session-monitor.ts` is the single abort-and-confirm boundary: it waits through the executor's bounded drain budget until no matching active run remains, serializes monitor settlement, and returns `termination_unconfirmed` when liveness cannot be disproven. Plans pause and Workflows block without retry on that outcome. Plan automatic retry, manual resume, and abandoned-child closure reuse the same fence. Principal, Plan lease, attempt ownership, and tool authorization are unchanged.

Controls: AGENT-04, OBS-01, DATA-01, REC-02. Owner: Agent Runtime / Orchestration. Severity: high. Rollback is the merged PR revert. Residual risk: an uncooperative child can force a fail-closed pause/block requiring later recovery, but cannot authorize an overlapping retry.

## 11.20 Metrics/KPI default reconciliation vault scope, August 5, 2026

A01/A02/S2 Metric and KPI definitions cross F04/F05 and B06/B11 when authenticated Business reads lazily reconcile canonical defaults. The prior account-only lazy guard and account-only slug indexes could cause one visible vault's defaults to satisfy another vault's reconciliation, while cleanup enumerated all visible rows before writable-scope deletion. That combination risked missing required per-vault defaults and attempting cross-vault mutation.

**Closed in source.** `server/metrics-routes.ts` keys lazy reconciliation by account and active vault and skips mutation without both. `server/metrics-seed.ts` default-denies execution without an active vault, filters canonical existence checks and untouched legacy cleanup to that vault, and continues to mutate only through principal-scoped storage APIs. `server/metrics-storage.ts` and `shared/models/metrics.ts` now enforce account-and-vault slug uniqueness instead of account-only uniqueness. Cleanup still requires exact untouched legacy signatures, preserving user-created or modified rows. Controls: DATA-01, DATA-04, AGENT-03. Owner: Business / Metrics. Severity: medium. SLA: closed in source on August 5, 2026. Rollback is the merged PR revert. Residual risk: modified legacy seeded rows remain intentionally because provenance is insufficient to distinguish them safely from user-owned data.

## 11.19 Legacy JSON tag-registry retirement, August 5, 2026

A02/S2 semantic tags cross F04/F05 and B06/B11 when authenticated TagService reads or mutations initialize. The retired compatibility path read the platform-global `system.tags.index` setting during user-scoped tag operations and attempted one-time ownership inference before copying entries into account/user-scoped SQL tables. Its ambiguity guard reduced immediate exposure, but retaining a global registry reader beside the canonical store preserved a second authority and a cross-account contamination risk.

**Closed in source.** `server/tag-service.ts` no longer imports system settings, models the legacy JSON registry, checks `legacy-json-v1`, or adopts global entries during canonical operations. The sole active store remains SQL `tags` and `tag_assignments`, scoped by `requireUserIdentity` and explicit account/user predicates. `server/file-storage/tags.ts` remains only as a caller compatibility facade and delegates every operation to TagService; it does not read or write JSON. The Companies/Theses `tags[]` backfill remains an explicitly separate, account-scoped migration. Controls: DATA-01, DATA-04, AGENT-03. Owner: Semantic Data / Tags. Severity: medium. SLA: closed in source on August 5, 2026. Rollback is the merged PR revert. Residual risk: the inert `system.tags.index` value and historical `tag_migrations` rows can remain until separately authorized data cleanup, but active code no longer reads either for legacy adoption.

## 11.17 Principal-scoped DOCX attachment reads, July 22, 2026

Uploaded DOCX files are A02/S2 data crossing F07/B07 and B10 from private object storage into a model-invoked read tool. The prior DOCX reader accepted only workspace paths, so canonical attachment paths failed even when upload and ACL state were valid. A naive repair using public URLs or direct raw storage keys would create an IDOR and disclosure risk.

**Closed in source.** `docx.read` now discriminates exact `/objects/...` entity paths from workspace paths. Object-backed reads require the current authenticated user principal, resolve through `ObjectStorageService`, enforce the existing object ACL with `ObjectPermission.READ`, cap the compressed input at 25 MB and declared uncompressed archive content at 100 MB, and parse the authorized buffer directly. Missing principal, foreign object, absent ACL, missing object, and oversized document all fail closed. No public URL, raw S3 key, temporary private copy, new route, provider, permission, or data class is introduced. Workspace reads and all DOCX write/edit/clone behavior remain unchanged. Controls: DATA-01, DATA-04, ING-01, AGENT-03. Security result: no open finding. Rollback is the merged PR revert; no schema or data mutation is involved.

### A74: App error telemetry discarded exception identity and over-collected navigation data

- **Status:** Resolved in code on 2026-08-06; pending production deployment verification.
- **Severity:** Medium observability and data-minimization defect. Global browser error and unhandled-rejection producers passed only structured objects to the shared logger, so canonical aggregation could not derive a stable error code or producer callsite. Their runtime context also included query strings, hashes, and full URLs that can contain S2 private identifiers or tokens.
- **Assets / boundaries:** A02/S2 client navigation state and runtime failures cross F02/F06 into authenticated client-log ingestion and B03 operational telemetry.
- **Threats:** Exception payloads or navigation parameters disclose private data in logs (STRIDE information disclosure / DATA-01), while flattened errors prevent reliable incident diagnosis and recovery (OBS-01).
- **Controls / owner:** The App boundary now normalizes every thrown or rejected value into a real `Error`, assigns deterministic `APP_WINDOW_ERROR` or `APP_UNHANDLED_REJECTION` codes when the producer supplied none, preserves the original cause, and records bounded operation, route pathname, source coordinates, and crash correlation. Query, hash, and full URL fields are removed. Owner: Client Platform / Reliability.
- **SLA:** Immediate. Closed in source with the production build required before merge.
- **Residual risk:** Third-party exception messages and stacks may still contain provider-authored sensitive text already governed by client-log redaction policy; source maps determine final bundle-to-source precision.

### A68: Vault administration page mounted a system-admin migration control for ordinary users

- **Status:** Resolved in code on 2026-08-05; pending production deployment verification.
- **Severity:** Medium availability and least-privilege defect. The server correctly denied the request, but the client exposed an inoperable privileged control and surfaced the resulting `system:write` denial on the ordinary Vault workflow.
- **Assets / data classes / boundaries:** Vault metadata and legacy migration state (S2); authenticated account principal boundary; browser-to-API boundary; system-administrator permission boundary.
- **Credible abuse / threat:** A non-admin authenticated user opening Vaults could invoke `GET /api/vaults/migration`. The deterministic server guard prevented privilege escalation, but the client generated unauthorized traffic and could mislead users into treating a privileged migration failure as a Vault creation failure (STRIDE: elevation attempt denied; availability/usability degradation; agentic risk from ambiguous authorization errors).
- **Canonical control and owner:** `server/routes/vault-routes.ts` remains the authoritative `system:write` enforcement boundary for migration operations. `client/src/pages/vaults-admin.tsx` now composes `VaultMigrationControls` only when `useAuth().hasPermission("system:write")` is true. Client gating is defense in depth and UX integrity, never authorization. Owner: Sharing/Vaults.
- **Evidence:** Production screenshot from 2026-08-05 showed `403 ... permission: system:write` on the Vaults page for an ordinary user; route inspection identified `VaultMigrationControls` as the only Vault-page caller of a `system:write`-guarded endpoint; impact analysis found a single low-risk contribution surface.
- **Residual risk / SLA:** Verify the repaired Vaults page with a non-admin principal after deployment and confirm that creating a Vault reaches `POST /api/vaults` without any migration request. Until deployment, the server continues to fail closed. Target: 2026-08-06.

### A69: Model provider failures lost deterministic source identity at the telemetry boundary

**Status:** Mitigated in code (2026-08-06); production build required before release. **Severity:** Medium. **Owner:** Agent Runtime / Security Program Owner. **SLA:** Before the next production release. **Assets/data:** A02/S0-S3 model prompts and responses, A03 session/run state, A07 telemetry, A08 provider availability. **Flows:** F02, F03, F12. **Boundaries:** B03, B04, B05. **Threat:** Provider, transport, or local adapter failures can contain untrusted payload text or non-`Error` thrown values. Logging only an interpolated message discards the producer stack and stable code, collapsing distinct failures into `ModelClient:Error:UNCLASSIFIED`; logging full provider payloads would instead risk exposing prompt, response, credential, or endpoint data. This is an observability-integrity and information-disclosure risk (STRIDE repudiation/information disclosure; LLM supply-chain payload risk). **Deterministic controls:** The canonical `chatCompletion` catch normalizes every escaped value through `enrichModelError`, preserves the existing bounded provider/client classification, and passes that structured `Error` to the shared logger so `recordApplicationError` derives a stable code and safe source callsite. Boundary inference-tracking failures normalize non-`Error` values, assign `INFERENCE_TRACKING_FAILED`, and provide a producer stack. Human-readable logs remain limited to existing provider/model/routing identifiers and normalized messages; provider payloads, prompts, responses, headers, credentials, and URLs are not added. Expected aborts remain debug-level and do not enter error telemetry. **Residual risk:** Upstream SDK messages may still include provider-authored text already permitted by existing logging policy; source maps can improve source precision but are not required for deterministic classification. Provider-specific adapters remain responsible for redacting secrets before constructing errors.

### A68: Business-owned aggregate metrics cross private source domains

**Status:** Closed in source; production build verified. **Severity:** High before controls; Low residual. **Owner:** Metrics / Identity and Data. **SLA:** Release-blocking for aggregate adapters. **Assets/data:** A01 account identity, S2 user memory claims and presence intervals, A03 Business/Metric ownership, A04 Vault memberships, A07 aggregate samples. **Flows:** named internal adapter reads private per-account source rows -> computes one aggregate -> writes one Business-owned Metric sample -> authenticated Business dashboard read. **Boundaries:** B01 authorization, B03 persistence, B06 observability, B08 named system aggregation. **Threat:** Treating each source account as the Metric owner created per-account series and encouraged an unverified viewer-time super-admin sum; alternatively exposing raw cross-account rows or allowing ordinary user authority to perform the aggregate would violate tenant isolation (STRIDE information disclosure/elevation/tampering; DATA-01/DATA-03/AGENT-04). **Deterministic controls:** Metric identity carries `business_id`; Metric visibility joins the canonical Business Vault membership predicate; one `(business_id, slug)` series is structurally unique. Exactly one Business per account may carry the database-owned `is_platform_instrument` capability, and the deterministic extracted Business receives it replay-safely by immutable ID, so renaming public/legal presentation cannot redirect or disable privileged telemetry. Adapter resolution, current-range authorization, Vault bootstrap, Metric backfill, and Business Plan backfill all derive from that capability rather than a mutable name. Named `internal/user-memory-v2` and `internal/hours-used-v2` background adapters alone read cross-account source tables and release only aggregate counts/time into the platform-instrument Business series. They return no user IDs, account IDs, claim text, intervals, or per-account breakdowns. UI/tool creation requires an independently visible/writable Business; viewing Business metrics never grants source-data access. The platform Business extraction and legacy per-account-series cleanup are exact, additive, and replay-safe. **Evidence:** `shared/models/metrics.ts`, `server/business-vault-access.ts`, `server/metrics-storage.ts`, `server/user-memory-metric.ts`, `server/hours-used.ts`, `client/src/pages/business-metrics.tsx`. **Business-owned page controls (2026-08-10):** Business Vault membership editing reuses the canonical full-set replacement mutation under `system:write`. Storage rejects empty sets and any archived, invisible, or cross-account Vault before atomically replacing scoped memberships; the client cannot widen visibility by naming arbitrary Vault IDs. Financial Models carry an immutable `business_id`, resolve only through the canonical Business visibility/writability predicate, and are unique per Business. KPI Business scope derives through the canonical KPI → Metric → Business chain rather than a duplicated client-owned identifier. **Residual risk:** An authorized viewer of the Mantra Business sees platform-wide totals and may infer coarse growth over time; future connector adapters remain untrusted external boundaries and must independently authenticate, minimize, and aggregate before writing samples. Raw source tables remain governed by their own principal scopes. Rollback may leave additive `business_id` columns and Business rows inert; reverting adapters restores prior projection but not deleted superseded telemetry samples.

### A67: Oversized tool output could bypass model-context and logging bounds

**Status:** Mitigated in code (2026-08-05); production build verified. **Severity:** High. **Owner:** Agent Runtime / Security Program Owner. **SLA:** Release-blocking for model-context injection paths. **Assets/data:** A02/S0-S3 retrieved and external tool content, A03 session/run state, A07 telemetry, A08 model-context availability. **Flows:** F02, F03, F06, F12. **Boundaries:** B03, B04, B05, B06, B14. **Threat:** A tool or external provider can return unexpectedly large, adversarial, secret-bearing, or malformed content; pagination is not a security boundary. Unbounded insertion can exhaust context, amplify prompt injection, or leak payloads through logs. Archive failure could otherwise cause raw fallback reinjection. **Deterministic controls:** Every model-facing tool result crosses the shared `maybeOffloadToolOutput` admission boundary. Inline admission is independently capped by both characters and estimated tokens; oversized/binary output is durably scoped through `ContentIndexer`, replaced with a compact canonical `@file` manifest, section handles, untrusted-content framing, and bounded retrieval guidance. Errors do not bypass admission. Archive failure fails closed with structured recovery metadata and no raw payload. Telemetry records only tool/action identifiers, disposition, size/token counts, run/session IDs, and artifact identity; it never records result content or secrets. `indexed_content` remains responsible for authenticated, user-scoped artifact retrieval. **Configuration:** `TOOL_OUTPUT_INLINE_CHAR_BUDGET=12000`, `TOOL_OUTPUT_INLINE_TOKEN_BUDGET=3000`, `TOOL_OUTPUT_PREVIEW_CHAR_BUDGET=1200`; environment overrides may only tune within the same central boundary. **Residual risk:** Token estimation is approximate; a future executor that bypasses the shared projector could evade admission; retrieved sections remain untrusted data and require the existing model instruction hierarchy and artifact authorization controls.

### 11.22 Goal Vault ownership boundary, August 6, 2026

**Status:** Closed in source.
**Severity:** High before controls; Low residual.
**Owner:** Goal System / Identity and Data.
**SLA:** Closed before Goal Vault ownership shipped.

**Assets/data:** A01 identity, A03 Goal state, A04 Vault/account relationships. **Flows:** authenticated Goal create, read, list, and update. **Boundaries:** B01 application authorization and B04 persistence.

**Threat:** A caller could omit Vault ownership or submit an inaccessible Vault ID, leaving Goals outside the Vault model or attaching Goal metadata to another account's context. Client-only selection would not protect tool, API, migration, or background creation paths.

**Deterministic controls:** `GoalStorage` is the canonical boundary. Every Goal resolves exactly one `vaultId`, defaults missing ownership to the principal's active authorized Vault (then first visible Vault), rejects inaccessible Vault IDs, validates persisted metadata during reads, and projects the field to every list consumer. The shared create/update schemas expose only one scalar Vault ID, so multi-membership cannot be represented.

**Evidence:** `shared/models/goals.ts`, `server/goal-storage.ts`, `client/src/components/goal-inline-details.tsx`, and `client/src/pages/goals.tsx`. Control mapping: AUTHZ-01, DATA-03, INPUT-01. **Residual risk:** Legacy Goals are assigned when next read rather than through an eager bulk migration; this is deterministic per principal and writes back immediately.

## 11.21 Git clone source-binding authority, August 6, 2026

Git clone crosses B03/B08 and F11 over A08/S0-S2: it invokes a credentialed external executable, retrieves provider-controlled source, and creates a session-owned mutable workspace. The prior contract accepted an arbitrary repository URL plus optional environment/connection hints, selected credentials from those coordinates, and could fall back to legacy Git credentials. A model, stale producer, or retrieved prompt could therefore request an unintended repository, create repository/environment mismatch, or cause credentials to be selected outside the canonical Platform source-binding identity.

**Closed in source.** Normal `git.clone` accepts no caller-owned repository coordinates and resolves exactly one active GitHub binding named `Mantra / Web / stage`. A positive `platformEnvironmentId` on `clone` or `clone_from_environment` selects that Platform Environment through the same resolver (the transitional `clone + platformEnvironmentId` shape is absorbed rather than rejected). Both paths derive URL, provider connection, credential, repository name, and branch exclusively from `environment_source_bindings`; stale caller-owned `url`, `directory`, or `branch` values are rejected, and dumped empty routing fields / non-positive IDs are treated as absence. Clone has no legacy credential fallback, uses `GIT_TERMINAL_PROMPT=0`, writes only to the session-owned `repos/` namespace, logs bounded source identity without credentials, removes partial destinations on failure, and reuses the immutable shared dependency image after clone. Controls: AUTHZ-01, EXEC-01, EXEC-02, DATA-03, AUDIT-01. Owner: Agent Runtime / Git Tools. Severity: Medium before fix; closed in source. **Residual risk:** Platform administrators can still misbind an environment or provider connection; provider compromise remains upstream; environment selection remains limited to the canonical Mantra default and an explicit Platform Environment ID.

## 11.23 Legacy chat Vault recovery and DB error classification, August 6, 2026

**Status:** Closed in source.
**Severity:** Medium before fix; Low residual.
**Owner:** Identity and Data / Database Runtime.
**SLA:** Immediate.

**Assets/data:** A01 identity, A03 Session/document ownership, A04 Vault/account relationships, A07 telemetry. **Flows:** boot schema convergence, Session Vault ownership repair, application-error aggregation. **Boundaries:** B03 persistence, B06 observability.

**Threat:** Boot recovery for legacy user chat documents joined `users.account_id`, a column that does not exist. PostgreSQL SQLSTATE `42703` aborted the recovery path, left nullable Vault ownership unrepaired, and emitted DB query-contract failures whose logger path discarded the original Error object. Aggregate identity then collapsed to message-token codes (`QUERY_CONTRACT_FAILED_AFTER`) without a stable producer code or SQLSTATE, delaying diagnosis (STRIDE: availability/repudiation). Logging full SQL parameters or provider messages would risk information disclosure.

**Deterministic controls:** `ensureVaults` recovers chat ownership only through the canonical `memberships` → personal `accounts` → default `vaults` join and never invents a `users.account_id` column. `server/db.ts` continues to throw the original Postgres error unchanged for callers, while query-contract and unexpected pool-connection error logs attach a telemetry-only `Error` with stable codes `QUERY_CONTRACT_FAILED` / `UNEXPECTED_POOL_CONNECTION_ERROR`, phase, lane/subsystem/label, pool counts, fingerprint, and SQLSTATE. EnsureVaults terminal failures classify as `FAILED_TO_ENSURE_VAULT_SCHEMA`. No SQL parameters, credentials, principal identity, or arbitrary error payloads are added. Controls: DATA-03, OBS-01, AUDIT-01.

**Evidence:** Live env #12 log `code=42703` on the chat Vault recovery UPDATE; `shared/schema.ts` users/memberships/accounts; `server/migrations/ensure-vaults.ts`; `server/db.ts` instrumentation. **Residual risk:** Pre-existing generic DB instrumentation may still emit bounded SQL text snippets (documented known inconsistency); this repair does not widen that surface. Historical UNCLASSIFIED aggregates cannot be retroactively enriched. Stage boot evidence (Issue 1786285870702) later showed residual legacy user chats can still violate `document_store_user_chat_vault_required` during VALIDATE (SQLSTATE 23514) and abort later EnsureVaults phases; the producer now defers VALIDATE while residuals remain and treats concurrent 23514 as a bounded warn so NOT VALID write enforcement continues without failing the whole vault ensure.

## 11.24 Pool-acquire timeout classification and bounded retry, August 6, 2026

**Status:** Closed in source.
**Severity:** Medium before fix; Low residual.
**Owner:** Identity and Data / Database Runtime.
**SLA:** Immediate.

**Assets/data:** A07 operational telemetry, A08 database availability. **Flows:** ordinary `pool.query` on general/voice lanes under load or brief network stalls. **Boundaries:** B03 persistence, B06 observability.

**Threat:** node-postgres pool acquisition failures (`timeout exceeded when trying to connect`, connect `ETIMEDOUT`) escaped as generic internal errors. Telemetry either kept Node codes or collapsed into `QUERY_CONTRACT_FAILED`, so operators could not distinguish pool pressure/connect stalls from SQL contract failures, and every acquire miss failed hard with no safe retry (STRIDE: availability/repudiation; OBS-01/DATA-02). Logging SQL parameters, credentials, or principal identity would add disclosure risk.

**Deterministic controls:** `isPoolAcquireTimeoutError` in `server/postgres-errors.ts` is the single detector. Instrumented general/voice `pool.query` classifies terminal acquire failures as `POOL_ACQUIRE_TIMEOUT` (not `QUERY_CONTRACT_FAILED` or raw `ETIMEDOUT`), retains lane/subsystem/label/pool counts/attempt/phase/SQLSTATE only, and retries acquire timeout **once** immediately because no application SQL has started. Statement timeouts, lock timeouts, constraint errors, and mid-query disconnects remain single-shot. Callers still receive the original thrown error. No SQL text parameters, secrets, or principal fields are added. Controls: OBS-01, AUDIT-01, DATA-02.

**Evidence:** Issue 1786061326715 (five concurrent connect-timeout exceptions); live DB op-summary pool samples; `server/db.ts` instrumentation; `server/postgres-errors.ts`. **Residual risk:** Auth-pool and dedicated-client paths remain outside this instrumentation; sustained pool exhaustion still fails after one retry and requires capacity/query diagnosis rather than larger blind retries.

## 11.28 Memory source Runtime admission and cognition invalidation, August 9, 2026

**Status:** Closed in source; production build required before release. **Severity:** Medium before controls; Low residual. **Owner:** Memory / Agent Runtime. **SLA:** Before the next production release. **Assets/data:** A02/S2 source content, vNext claims and provenance, A03 Runtime/source lifecycle state, A07 invalidation evidence. **Flows:** settled Session/Library/Drive source ingestion into native Runtime and model-authored cognition observations into context. **Boundaries:** B03 persistence, B06 observability, B08 autonomous execution. **Threat:** Multiple replicas could read the same settled unbound source before either persisted its Runtime relation. Runtime idempotency prevented duplicate Runs, but intake had no atomic cross-replica ownership boundary, causing redundant authorization/enqueue work and making the queue-to-Run projection temporarily ambiguous (STRIDE tampering/repudiation/denial-of-service; DATA-03/AGENT-04). The canonical observation mutation also published the same invalidation twice, producing duplicate process-local side effects after one durable claim write. **Deterministic controls:** `bindNextSettledSourceRuntime()` now selects one settled row with `FOR UPDATE SKIP LOCKED`, restores the exact owner principal, executes only bounded idempotent Runtime enqueue database work, and persists `runtime_run_id` plus the exact source version in the same transaction. The transaction rolls back both records on failure; extraction/model work remains outside it. Runtime remains the sole execution-capacity and attempt-lease authority, and graph mutation still revalidates the exact source-version/attempt fence. `recordMetacognitiveObservation()` continues to mutate only through `applyObservation()` and emits one ambient-principal-scoped invalidation after commit. **Evidence:** `server/memory/vnext-source-queue.ts`, `server/memory/vnext-source-poller.ts`, `server/runtime/runtime-storage.ts`, `server/memory/metacognitive-observations.ts`, and updated memory/server AGENTS contracts. **Residual risk:** A transaction can hold one source-row lock during Runtime authorization and enqueue queries, bounded by the ordinary database statement ceiling; no model/provider/network work occurs under that lock. Process-local invalidation remains best-effort while PostgreSQL claims/provenance remain canonical.

## 11.27 Chat attachment storage integrity, August 9, 2026

**Status:** Closed in source; production build required before release. **Severity:** High before controls; Low residual. **Owner:** Identity and Data / Object Storage. **SLA:** Before the next production release. **Assets/data:** A01 authenticated user/account/Vault identity, S2/S3 uploaded attachment bytes and extracted text, object ACL rows, and ephemeral multipart files. **Flows:** authenticated chat multipart upload to private object storage and later `/objects/uploads/*` reads. **Boundaries:** B01 authorization, B03 persistence, B06 observability. **Threat:** The chat upload route independently wrote object bytes and ACL metadata, marked attachments public, used synchronous whole-file reads on the request path, and returned an ephemeral local path after object persistence failure even though cleanup then deleted that file. A failed or partial write could therefore create unowned bytes, a broken durable reference, cross-user disclosure, or event-loop pressure (STRIDE information disclosure/tampering/denial-of-service; DATA-01/DATA-03/OBS-01). **Deterministic controls:** Chat uploads now require an exact user/account principal and delegate to `ObjectStorageService.uploadObjectEntity(...)`, the canonical Vault-keyed server upload boundary. That boundary writes bytes, persists a private owner/account/Vault ACL, verifies object existence and byte length, and compensates any partial ACL/object state before rethrowing. The route reads asynchronously, always cleans its temp file, fails the request when durable storage fails, and returns only a verified `/objects/uploads/*` path. No local fallback or public visibility remains. **Evidence:** `server/integrations/chat/routes.ts`, `server/object_storage/objectStorage.ts`, and `server/AGENTS.md`. **Residual risk:** Object storage and PostgreSQL cannot share one atomic transaction; compensating deletion is best-effort and emits bounded failure telemetry if cleanup itself fails. Existing historical public ACLs and broken local attachment references are not rewritten by this patch.

## 11.26 Password-signup provenance and New Users aggregate, August 8, 2026

**Status:** Closed in source; production build required before release. **Severity:** High if qualification were inferred or identity rows exposed; Low residual. **Owner:** Identity and Data / Metrics. **SLA:** Before New Users metric release. **Assets/data:** A01 identity, S2 account metadata, S3 password credentials, A07 operational metrics. **Flows:** password registration and recap claim into the User store; authenticated Metrics range sampling. **Boundaries:** B01 authentication/authorization, B03 persistence, B06 observability. **Threat:** A password hash or generic User row does not prove password signup: invite shells, initial administrators, resets, imports, and compatibility rows may carry credentials without crossing registration. Heuristic qualification would corrupt growth reporting, while returning IDs, emails, or exact per-user rows would enable account enumeration (STRIDE information disclosure/tampering; agentic provider-field forgery). **Deterministic controls:** Authentication stamps nullable `users.password_signup_at` only in canonical public registration, invite completion, and recap provisional-identity claim transactions. Invite completion uses activation time, not shell `created_at`. Initial setup, invite-shell provisioning, resets, and password changes do not stamp it. Historical rows remain NULL and are never guessed from hashes, roles, or timestamps. The `system:read`-guarded Metrics range endpoint/tool executes one bounded half-open aggregate query and returns only `newUsers` plus explicit partial coverage (`availableFrom`, historical rows unclassified); it never returns user IDs or emails. Provider-controlled profile fields cannot write the discriminant. The additive nullable migration is rollback-safe. **Residual risk:** Authorized system operators can observe coarse platform-wide signup counts; pre-provenance history remains intentionally incomplete.

## 11.29 Voice provider and configuration boundary, August 9, 2026

**Assets and data classes:** authenticated voice/chat Sessions (S2), voice transcripts and context prompts (S2), ElevenLabs credentials and callback topology (S3), provider configuration and cloned voice samples (S2/S3), pronunciation configuration (S2).

**Flows and trust boundaries:** authenticated browser → voice start/configuration routes; ElevenLabs signed custom-LLM callback → capability route → exact active voice lease → restored owner Principal; server → fixed-origin ElevenLabs APIs; client diagnostic beacon → bounded server logging. Voice remains non-installable Core interface infrastructure; this change does not add or alter Mod ownership.

**Verified abuse cases / threats:**

- **Spoofing / elevation of privilege:** ElevenLabs configuration, voice-clone, pronunciation, callback-base mutation, context-preview, and legacy archive routes were registered without complete route-level authentication and named-permission gates. An unauthenticated caller could reach secret-backed provider operations or user-context/storage paths; a low-authority authenticated caller could mutate account-wide voice configuration.
- **Information disclosure:** the context-preview and recent-diagnostics routes could expose personal prompt context, tool inventory, deployment callback topology, or voice/session identifiers without an authenticated Principal.
- **Denial of service / external boundary exhaustion:** the ElevenLabs adapter used direct unbounded `fetch` calls and fully buffered untrusted error bodies. A slow or oversized provider response could retain request resources and amplify log/memory pressure.
- **Integrity / stale compatibility:** product configuration still advertised a retired V3 webhook secret and absent `voice-v3` engine semantics, inviting operators to rotate inert state while believing it changed runtime authority.

**Canonical controls and owner:** `requireAuth` plus central `requirePermission("system:read"|"system:write")` gate voice provider configuration and pronunciation; signed callbacks retain HMAC capability validation and exact durable lease/Principal restoration; `server/integrations/provider-http.ts` owns ElevenLabs request deadlines and bounded error-body reads; `server/voice/` remains the single custom-LLM call engine; the secret catalog no longer advertises the retired V3-only secret. Legacy `voice_session` archives remain compatibility data only, never live call authority.

**Evidence / status:** fixed in this change. All ElevenLabs requests cross the shared abort-capable transport; error bodies are bounded; secret-backed provider/configuration routes require named permissions; context and archive compatibility routes require authenticated Principals; stale V3 UI/catalog/docs are removed. Required production build is the release gate.

**Residual risk and migration constraint:** legacy `voice_session` documents and their detail UI still require `voice-session-engine.ts` plus `/api/voice/sessions/save`; they cannot be deleted until stored compatibility data and UI are migrated. The historical `handleV25CustomLLM`/session-state names remain import/provider compatibility identities, not a second engine. Webhook override cache remains per process, so replicas converge on reload/restart rather than a distributed cache invalidation. Severity after controls: **low**. Owner: Voice Platform. SLA: migrate or reaffirm retained archive compatibility by **2026-09-09**.

## 11.30 Mobile native debug authority and bounded transport, August 9, 2026

**Status:** Closed in source; production build required before release. **Severity:** High before controls; Low residual. **Owner:** Mobile / Identity and Access. **SLA:** Before the next production release. **Assets/data:** authenticated Session cookies (S3), native Meta DAT device control and camera output (S2/S3), glasses session records (S2), capture uploads (S2), mobile telemetry (A07). **Flows:** Expo/WebView cookie authentication, mobile native HTTP, remote DAT command polling/results, camera capture upload, startup telemetry. **Boundaries:** B01 authentication/authorization, B02 external/native input, B05 device execution, B06 observability.

**Threat:** The registered `/api/mobile/dat-debug/*` routes accepted command enqueue, polling, result mutation, and diagnostic reads without authentication or named permission checks. Any network caller could therefore trigger or consume native-device actions including registration, connection, permission prompts, and camera capture (STRIDE spoofing/elevation/information disclosure/denial of service). Mobile API, glasses-session, and capture adapters also opened direct requests without cancellation deadlines, while startup telemetry retained a separate hard-coded production host; a stalled network could hold work indefinitely and backend selection could split across state owners.

**Deterministic controls:** Every DAT debug control/read route now requires `requireAuth` plus central `build:write`; the independently user-owned capture-upload route retains authenticated Principal scope and canonical private object/Media persistence. `mobile/src/lib/network.ts` is the single cookie-authenticated native transport, resolves `Config.SERVER_URL` at request time, composes caller abort with a 15-second deadline, and never automatically retries ambiguous mutations. JSON API, glasses-session, and capture adapters cross that boundary. Startup telemetry derives the same backend from `Config`, retains its shorter best-effort deadline and bounded replay queue, and no longer contains a parallel host fallback. The Expo module/plugin registrations and native event/deep-link callbacks remain intact because current config/import evidence proves them reachable.

**Evidence:** `server/routes/index.ts`, `server/routes/mobile-dat-debug.ts`, `mobile/src/lib/network.ts`, `mobile/src/lib/api.ts`, `mobile/src/lib/glasses-session-api.ts`, `mobile/src/services/dat-bridge-service.ts`, `mobile/src/lib/startup-telemetry.ts`, `mobile/app.config.js`, and `mobile/modules/agent-native/expo-module.config.json`. **Residual risk:** DAT debug commands remain process-local and at-most-once after poll consumption; interruption can lose a debug result. This is diagnostic state, not durable product state, and now requires build authority. Session-cookie availability still depends on the iOS shared cookie jar; authentication failures fail closed at server routes.

## 11.31 Canonical schema-convergence authority, August 9, 2026

**Status:** Closed in source; production build required before release. **Severity:** High before controls; Low residual. **Owner:** Identity and Data / Database Runtime. **SLA:** Before the next production release. **Assets/data:** A03 persistent application state, A04 account/Vault ownership schema, A07 boot and migration evidence, A08 database availability. **Flows:** process startup, DB Sync restore, route registration, and post-ready index maintenance. **Boundaries:** B03 persistence, B06 observability, B08 privileged autonomous/operational execution.

**Threat:** Schema mutation authority was distributed across module-evaluation promises, `server/index.ts`, `runSchemaBootstrap()`, subsystem ensure functions, Library route registration, DB Sync restore, and post-ready index maintenance. A rolling deployment or partial failure could therefore accept requests with an incomplete contract, run destructive legacy cleanup as a side effect of registering HTTP routes, reorder ownership constraints across replicas, or obscure which actor changed deployed state (STRIDE tampering/repudiation/denial of service; DATA-03/OBS-01). A JavaScript timeout around SQL is not cancellation and could let a nominally timed-out compatibility heal mutate later.

**Deterministic controls:** `server/schema-convergence.ts` is the one deployed-schema composition owner. It runs required additive/idempotent phases before readiness, owns ordering/fatality, and emits bounded structured phase start/completion/failure evidence without SQL, parameters, principal identity, or row content. `runSchemaBootstrap()` and subsystem ensure functions are delegated implementation actors only. DB Sync requests baseline-table convergence through the same boundary. `registerLibraryRoutes()` is DDL-free; its retained Library compatibility schema and Notes adoption run only in the explicit boot phase. Concurrent document-search index replacement remains a named post-ready delegation because PostgreSQL requires an independent session; it retains advisory locking, build-before-retire, bounded operational verification, and visible retries. Immutable migration files remain additive rollback/history artifacts and Drizzle declarations remain contracts, never ambient runtime owners.

**Evidence:** `server/schema-convergence.ts`, `server/index.ts`, `server/library-schema.ts`, `server/routes/library.ts`, `server/routes/brain.ts`, root/server `AGENTS.md`. **Residual risk:** `runSchemaBootstrap()` remains a large compatibility implementation body and some old `heal(...)` blocks preserve non-fatal rolling-repair semantics with non-canceling JavaScript timers. They are now ordered and observable beneath one owner but should be decomposed only through additive consumer migrations; uncertainty is not grounds for deleting historical repair paths. Post-ready indexes can be temporarily unavailable after readiness, and search must continue to surface truthful degraded/failure state.

## 11.25 Intentional silent session completion vs empty_response, August 6, 2026

**Status:** Closed in source.
**Severity:** Medium before fix; Low residual.
**Owner:** Agent Runtime / Communications.
**SLA:** Immediate.

**Assets/data:** A02/S2 private Session and tool evidence, A03 durable assistant/Session state, A07 terminal audit evidence. **Flows:** autonomous Skill runs that intentionally end via `session.end` / `set_status=saved` without final assistant narration (e.g. enrich-email). **Boundaries:** B03 persistence, B06 observability, B08 model/tool loop.

**Threat:** After successful tool work and intentional terminal session status, the executor continued the model loop. Provider empty `end_turn` responses were then classified `empty_response` degradation even though the skill had already completed its contract. That produced false-red EmailEnrichment skill runs, wasted empty-final-turn retries, and obscured whether enrichment tool work succeeded (STRIDE: tampering/repudiation/availability analogue; DATA-01/AGENT-04/OBS-01). Expanding diagnostics with transcript or email bodies would add disclosure risk.

**Deterministic controls:** Successful `session.end` and `set_status=saved` emit controlled continuation `session_complete`. `AgentExecutor` stops the loop on that discriminant (and defensively when a pending saved session end already exists), sets `intentionallyCompletedSession`, and excludes that path from `empty_response` / empty-final-turn retries. True empty completions without intentional terminal status remain degraded. Terminal metadata stays bounded (reason/counts/IDs only); no transcript, tool payload, or email content is added. Principal/Vault scope, tool authority, and provider routing are unchanged. Controls: OBS-01, AUDIT-01, AGENT-04.

**Evidence:** Issue 1786061327389; live logs showing enrich-email-class runs with `degradationReason=empty_response`, `emptyFinalTurnRetries=2`, nonzero toolCalls, `contentLen=0`; `server/agent-executor.ts`; `server/bridge-tools.ts` session end/status handlers. **Residual risk:** Skills that neither call `session.end`/`set_status=saved` nor produce final text still degrade as `empty_response` by design; provider empty turns after incomplete work remain visible.

## 11.32 Business-scoped operating budgets, August 11, 2026

**Status:** Closed in source; production build required before release. **Severity:** High without controls; Low residual. **Owner:** Business / Identity and Data. **SLA:** Before the Budgets surface is released. **Assets/data:** A01 authenticated principal and Business/Vault identity, A03/S2 department/category/line-item budget amounts, A07 mutation evidence. **Flows:** authenticated Business page or model-originated Business tool → canonical Budget storage → principal-scoped PostgreSQL aggregate. **Boundaries:** B01 authentication/authorization, B03 persistence, B06 observability, B08 model/tool authority.

**Threat:** A caller could supply another Business ID or race concurrent hierarchy edits to read or overwrite private operating plans (STRIDE spoofing, information disclosure, tampering, denial of service). Model- or browser-controlled nested payloads could also create unbounded documents or persist invalid monthly amount or currency state.

**Deterministic controls:** The Business Mod owns both the route group and the `business` Agent tool, so disabling the Mod removes both projection and execution. Reads require `system:read`; mutations require `system:write`, enforced independently by HTTP middleware or deterministic Agent authority before the shared storage boundary. Tool actions are explicit, use stable hierarchy IDs returned by `get_budget`, keep inspection read-only, create the aggregate only through the first mutation, and classify destructive deletes above ordinary internal mutations. `business-budget-storage.ts` independently composes canonical Business Vault visibility/writability with principal-owned Budget scope on every read/write, and one canonical compatibility key permits only one active hypothetical-month aggregate per Business. Mutations accept the same bounded discriminated Zod contract from UI/API and tool callers, serialize under a transaction-scoped Business advisory lock, and validate the complete resulting Department → Category → Line item document before persistence. PostgreSQL constrains currency; the monthly total is derived from validated non-negative integer cents and never accepted as caller-owned truth. Schema convergence is additive and pre-readiness. Logs retain bounded status/error context only—never Business names, line items, or amounts.

**Evidence:** `shared/models/business-budgets.ts`, `shared/schema.ts`, `server/business-budget-schema.ts`, `server/business-budget-storage.ts`, `server/business-budget-routes.ts`, `server/tools/business-plans.ts`, `server/agent-authority.ts`, and the Business Mod registry contribution. **Residual risk:** The first release supports USD and one normalized hypothetical month only; expanding currency or adding period-specific scenarios requires a deliberate contract migration. Delete actions are immediate and rely on PostgreSQL rollback/backup rather than an object-level trash lifecycle. Disabling the Business Mod removes route/UI/tool projection while retaining durable budget state for replay-safe reinstall.

## 11.35 Business-owned Pricing catalog, August 18, 2026

**Status:** Closed in source; production build required before release. **Severity:** High without controls; Low residual. **Owner:** Business / Identity and Data. **SLA:** Before the Pricing surface is released. **Assets/data:** A01 authenticated principal and Business/Vault identity, A03/S2 package prices and includes, A07 mutation evidence. **Flows:** authenticated Business page or model-originated Business tool → canonical Pricing storage → principal-scoped PostgreSQL catalog. **Boundaries:** B01 authentication/authorization, B03 persistence, B06 observability, B08 model/tool authority.

**Threat:** A caller could supply another Business ID or race concurrent catalog edits to read or overwrite private commercial prices (STRIDE spoofing, information disclosure, tampering). Model- or browser-controlled payloads could invent a fourth package, store year-one monthly as a second price, or persist unbounded essay fields as if they were sellable numbers.

**Deterministic controls:** The Business Mod owns the `/business/pricing` route, navbar item, and `business` tool actions, so disabling the Mod removes projection and execution. Reads require `system:read`; mutations require `system:write`, enforced independently by HTTP middleware or deterministic Agent authority before the shared storage boundary. `BusinessPricingStorage` independently composes canonical Business Vault visibility/writability with principal-owned Pricing scope on every read/write. One unique Business row is the catalog; first get seeds the code-owned Max / Max+ / Factory+ ladder. Mutations accept the same bounded Zod contract from UI/API and tool callers, serialize under a transaction-scoped Business advisory lock, and reject unknown package keys. Year-one monthly is derived. Logs retain bounded status/error context only—never package amounts.

**Evidence:** `shared/models/business-pricing.ts`, `shared/schema.ts`, `server/business-pricing-schema.ts`, `server/business-pricing-storage.ts`, `server/business-pricing-routes.ts`, `server/tools/business-plans.ts`, `server/agent-authority.ts`, and the Business Mod registry contribution. **Residual risk:** Disabling the Business Mod removes route/UI/tool projection while retaining durable catalog state for replay-safe reinstall. Forecast remap remains a later consumer and must not copy these numbers into enterprise* assumption keys.

## 11.33 Product Vault placement, August 13, 2026

**Status:** Closed in source; production build required before release. **Severity:** High without controls; Low residual. **Owner:** Build Products. **SLA:** Immediate.

**Assets/data:** A01 principal/account-scoped Product intent in `products` (S2), A06 Vault identity. `products.vault_id` is scalar placement, not a second Product authority.

**Flow/threat:** authenticated Products UI or `platforms` `create_product`/`update_product` → `product-storage.ts` → `products.vault_id`. A caller-supplied foreign or archived Vault ID would pin Product intent to another account's container (STRIDE tampering/information disclosure; DATA-01/IAM-01). Filtering Products by Vault would hide unassigned intent and invent a second visibility owner beside principal/account scope.

**Deterministic controls:** `product-storage.ts` remains the sole ordinary mutation boundary. Create defaults omitted Vault to the current Principal's `activeVaultId`; explicit `null` stays unassigned and visible. Create/update independently require a live same-account Vault when a Vault ID is present. Product list visibility stays principal/account scoped and is not Vault-filtered. Named `build:write`, Build composition, and existing Product routes remain independent. Logs retain bounded status/error context only.

**Evidence:** `shared/models/platforms.ts`, `server/product-storage.ts`, `server/schema-bootstrap.ts`, `server/AGENTS.md` Build Product boundary, `client/src/pages/products.tsx`. **Residual risk:** existing Products are backfilled to the account default Vault when one exists; a Product with no default Vault remains unassigned until an owner sets one. Revert the column, heal, storage check, and this finding together.

## 11.34 Identity stock Metrics, August 14, 2026

**Status:** Closed in source; production build required before release. **Severity:** High without controls; Low residual. **Owner:** Core Metrics + Identity. **SLA:** Immediate.

**Assets/data:** A01 identity `accounts` / `users` rows (S1 operational counts), A07 derived Metric/KPI observations. Responses contain counts only.

**Flow/threat:** authenticated Metrics/KPI read → `sampleIdentityStock` / `sampleIdentityRange` → query-time overlay on durable `accounts` and `registered-users` definitions. Binding those stocks to Forecast Starting Accounts / Starting Users would treat every active identity account as a paying Max logo (STRIDE tampering / data-integrity; DATA-01/OBS-01). Writing manual samples would invent a second source of truth.

**Deterministic controls:** Identity tables remain the sole source. Accounts count `status = active` only. Users count distinct users with a membership on an active (`status = active`) Account — Account status is the sole lifecycle discriminant, since users carry no archived/suspended field, so a user whose only Accounts are archived or suspended is excluded. Slugs stay off the Forecast catalog (`paying-accounts`, `users`, `new-users`) and off presence adapters (`current-users`, `active-users`). Overlay is query-time and writes no `metric_samples`. Existing Metrics authorization, platform-instrument gate, and Business visibility remain independent. Model Starting Accounts / Starting Users stay unbound.

**Evidence:** `server/identity-metrics.ts`, `server/metrics-storage.ts`, `server/AGENTS.md` Metrics integrity boundary. **Residual risk:** identity stock is not a commercial cohort. Binding it later still requires a paying-account object that does not exist.
